Trace the retire/archive predicate.\n- **The model_version root-cause fix (DR-115 provenance)**: confirm model_version now carries the RESPONSE model identity, not the request id, and the fix corrected the persistence AND return mapping (not just silenced the test). {"parentUuid":"96c183e1-8f21-4544-94b3-4045b049ea26","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a5a3f8308606d29b3","attachment":{"type":"skill_listing","content":"- heartbeat-protocol: Claude node contract for DebateAI Graph Spine v2. The Main Orchestrator (Claude-Router seat) contract; thin loader over the repo spine. All agent launches use /goal.\n- commit-commands:clean_gone: Cleans up all git branches marked as [gone] (branches that have been deleted on the remote but still exist locally), including removing associated worktrees.\n- commit-commands:commit-push-pr: Commit, push, and open a PR\n- commit-commands:commit: Create a git commit\n- pdf-viewer:annotate: Collaboratively annotate a PDF — propose markup, review together, iterate\n- pdf-viewer:fill-form: Fill PDF form fields interactively with live visual feedback\n- pdf-viewer:open: Open a PDF in the interactive viewer\n- pdf-viewer:sign: Place a signature or initials image on a PDF\n- frontend-design:frontend-design: Guidance for distinctive, intentional visual design when building new UI or reshaping an existing one. Helps with aesthetic direction, typography, and making choices that don't read as templated defaults.\n- mattpocock-skills:diagnosing-bugs: Diagnosis loop for hard bugs and performance regressions. Use when the user says \"diagnose\"/\"debug this\", or reports something broken/throwing/failing/slow.\n- mattpocock-skills:tdd: Test-driven development. Use when the user wants to build features or fix bugs test-first, mentions \"red-green-refactor\", or wants integration tests.\n- mattpocock-skills:prototype: Build a throwaway prototype to answer a design question. Use when the user wants to sanity-check whether a state model or logic feels right, or explore what a UI should look like.\n- mattpocock-skills:research: Investigate a question against high-trust primary sources and capture the findings as a Markdown file in the repo. Use when the user wants a topic researched, docs or API facts gathered, or reading legwork delegated to a background agent.\n- mattpocock-skills:domain-modeling: Build and sharpen a project's domain model. Use when the user wants to pin down domain terminology or a ubiquitous language, record an architectural decision, or when another skill needs to maintain the domain model.\n- mattpocock-skills:codebase-design: Shared vocabulary for designing deep modules. Use when the user wants to design or improve a module's interface, find deepening opportunities, decide where a seam goes, make code more testable or AI-navigable, or when another skill needs the deep-module vocabulary.\n- mattpocock-skills:code-review: Review the changes since a fixed point (commit, branch, tag, or merge-base) along two axes — Standards (does the code follow this repo's documented coding standards?) and Spec (does the code match what the originating issue/PRD asked for?). Runs both reviews in parallel sub-agents and reports them side by side. Use when the user wants to review a branch, a PR, work-in-progress changes, or asks to \"review since X\".\n- mattpocock-skills:resolving-merge-conflicts: Use when you need to resolve an in-progress git merge/rebase conflict.\n- mattpocock-skills:grilling: Grill the user relentlessly about a plan, decision, or idea. Use when the user wants to stress-test their thinking, or uses any 'grill' trigger phrases.\n- superpowers:brainstorming: You MUST use this before any creative work - creating features, building components, adding functionality, or modifying behavior. Explores user intent, requirements and design before implementation.\n- superpowers:dispatching-parallel-agents: Use when facing 2+ independent tasks that can be worked on without shared state or sequential dependencies\n- superpowers:executing-plans: Use when you have a written implementation plan to execute in a separate session with review checkpoints\n- superpowers:finishing-a-development-branch: Use when implementation is complete, all tests pass, and you need to decide how to integrate the work\n- superpowers:receiving-code-review: Use when receiving code review feedback, before implementing suggestions, especially if feedback seems unclear or technically questionable - requires technical rigor and verification, not performative agreement or blind implementation\n- superpowers:requesting-code-review: Use when completing tasks, implementing major features, or before merging to verify work meets requirements\n- superpowers:subagent-driven-development: Use when executing implementation plans with independent tasks in the current session\n- superpowers:systematic-debugging: Use when encountering any bug, test failure, or unexpected behavior, before proposing fixes\n- superpowers:test-driven-development: Use when implementing any feature or bugfix, before writing implementation code\n- superpowers:using-git-worktrees: Use when starting feature work that needs isolation from current workspace or before executing implementation plans - ensures an isolated workspace exists via native tools or git worktree fallback\n- superpowers:using-superpowers: Use when starting any conversation - establishes how to find and use skills, requiring skill invocation before ANY response including clarifying questions\n- superpowers:verification-before-completion: Use when about to claim work is complete, fixed, or passing, before committing or creating PRs - requires running verification commands and confirming output before making any success claims; evidence before assertions always\n- superpowers:writing-plans: Use when you have a spec or requirements for a multi-step task, before touching code\n- superpowers:writing-skills: Use when creating new skills, editing existing skills, or verifying skills work before deployment\n- pdf-viewer:view-pdf: Interactive PDF viewer. Use when the user wants to open, show, or view a PDF and collaborate on it visually — annotate, highlight, stamp, fill form fields, place signature/initials, or review markup together. Not for summarization or text extraction (use native Read instead).\n- productivity:memory-management: Two-tier memory system that makes Claude a true workplace collaborator. Decodes shorthand, acronyms, nicknames, and internal language so Claude understands requests like a colleague would. CLAUDE.md for working memory, memory/ directory for the full knowledge base.\n- productivity:start: Initialize the productivity system and open the dashboard. Use when setting up the plugin for the first time, bootstrapping working memory from your existing task list, or decoding the shorthand (nicknames, acronyms, project codenames) you use in your todos.\n- productivity:task-management: Simple task management using a shared TASKS.md file. Reference this when the user asks about their tasks, wants to add/complete tasks, or needs help tracking commitments.\n- productivity:update: Sync tasks and refresh memory from your current activity. Use when pulling new assignments from your project tracker into TASKS.md, triaging stale or overdue tasks, filling memory gaps for unknown people or projects, or running a comprehensive scan to catch todos buried in chat and email.\n- cowork-plugin-management:cowork-plugin-customizer: Customize a Claude Code plugin for a specific organization's tools and workflows. Use when: customize plugin, set up plugin, configure plugin, tailor plugin, adjust plugin settings, customize plugin connectors, customize plugin skill, tweak plugin, modify plugin configuration.\n- cowork-plugin-management:create-cowork-plugin: Guide users through creating a new plugin from scratch in a cowork session. Use when users want to create a plugin, build a plugin, make a new plugin, develop a plugin, scaffold a plugin, start a plugin from scratch, or design a plugin. This skill requires Cowork mode with access to the outputs directory for delivering the final .plugin file.\n- engineering:architecture: Create or evaluate an architecture decision record (ADR). Use when choosing between technologies (e.g., Kafka vs SQS), documenting a design decision with trade-offs and consequences, reviewing a system design proposal, or designing a new component from requirements and constraints.\n- engineering:code-review: Review code changes for security, performance, and correctness. Trigger with a PR URL or diff, \"review this before I merge\", \"is this code safe?\", or when checking a change for N+1 queries, injection risks, missing edge cases, or error handling gaps.\n- engineering:debug: Structured debugging session — reproduce, isolate, diagnose, and fix. Trigger with an error message or stack trace, \"this works in staging but not prod\", \"something broke after the deploy\", or when behavior diverges from expected and the cause isn't obvious.\n- engineering:deploy-checklist: Pre-deployment verification checklist. Use when about to ship a release, deploying a change with database migrations or feature flags, verifying CI status and approvals before going to production, or documenting rollback triggers ahead of time.\n- engineering:documentation: Write and maintain technical documentation. Trigger with \"write docs for\", \"document this\", \"create a README\", \"write a runbook\", \"onboarding guide\", or when the user needs help with any form of technical writing — API docs, architecture docs, or operational runbooks.\n- engineering:incident-response: Run an incident response workflow — triage, communicate, and write postmortem. Trigger with \"we have an incident\", \"production is down\", an alert that needs severity assessment, a status update mid-incident, or when writing a blameless postmortem after resolution.\n- engineering:standup: Generate a standup update from recent activity. Use when preparing for daily standup, summarizing yesterday's commits and PRs and ticket moves, formatting work into yesterday/today/blockers, or structuring a few rough notes into a shareable update.\n- engineering:system-design: Design systems, services, and architectures. Trigger with \"design a system for\", \"how should we architect\", \"system design for\", \"what's the right architecture for\", or when the user needs help with API design, data modeling, or service boundaries.\n- engineering:tech-debt: Identify, categorize, and prioritize technical debt. Trigger with \"tech debt\", \"technical debt audit\", \"what should we refactor\", \"code health\", or when the user asks about code quality, refactoring priorities, or maintenance backlog.\n- engineering:testing-strategy: Design test strategies and test plans. Trigger with \"how should we test\", \"test strategy for\", \"write tests for\", \"test plan\", \"what tests do we need\", or when the user needs help with testing approaches, coverage, or test architecture.\n- design:accessibility-review: Run a WCAG 2.1 AA accessibility audit on a design or page. Trigger with \"audit accessibility\", \"check a11y\", \"is this accessible?\", or when reviewing a design for color contrast, keyboard navigation, touch target size, or screen reader behavior before handoff.\n- design:design-critique: Get structured design feedback on usability, hierarchy, and consistency. Trigger with \"review this design\", \"critique this mockup\", \"what do you think of this screen?\", or when sharing a Figma link or screenshot for feedback at any stage from exploration to final polish.\n- design:design-handoff: Generate developer handoff specs from a design. Use when a design is ready for engineering and needs a spec sheet covering layout, design tokens, component props, interaction states, responsive breakpoints, edge cases, and animation details.\n- design:design-system: Audit, document, or extend your design system. Use when checking for naming inconsistencies or hardcoded values across components, writing documentation for a component's variants, states, and accessibility notes, or designing a new pattern that fits the existing system.\n- design:research-synthesis: Synthesize user research into themes, insights, and recommendations. Use when you have interview transcripts, survey results, usability test notes, support tickets, or NPS responses that need to be distilled into patterns, user segments, and prioritized next steps.\n- design:user-research: Plan, conduct, and synthesize user research. Trigger with \"user research plan\", \"interview guide\", \"usability test\", \"survey design\", \"research questions\", or when the user needs help with any aspect of understanding their users through research.\n- design:ux-copy: Write or review UX copy — microcopy, error messages, empty states, CTAs. Trigger with \"write copy for\", \"what should this button say?\", \"review this error message\", or when naming a CTA, wording a confirmation dialog, filling an empty state, or writing onboarding text.\n- anthropic-skills:consolidate-memory: Reflective pass over your memory files — merge duplicates, fix stale facts, prune the index.\n- anthropic-skills:docx: Use this skill whenever the user wants to create, read, edit, or manipulate Word documents (.docx files) or Word templates (.dotx files). Triggers include: any mention of 'Word doc', 'word document', '.docx', '.dotx', or requests to produce professional documents with formatting like tables of contents, headings, page numbers, or letterheads. Also use when extracting or reorganizing content from .docx or .dotx files, inserting or replacing images in documents, performing find-and-replace in Word files, working with tracked changes or comments, or converting content into a polished Word document. If the user asks for a 'report', 'memo', 'letter', 'template', or similar deliverable as a Word or .docx file, use this skill. Do NOT use for PDFs, spreadsheets, Google Docs, or general coding tasks unrelated to document generation.\n- anthropic-skills:explain-usage: Explain where this session's tokens went, with one simple chart in plain language. Use when the user says things like \"explain my usage\", \"where did my tokens go\", or asks for a usage breakdown.\n- anthropic-skills:morning: Render the user's morning brief as a styled HTML artifact, or set it up as a recurring weekday task. Use only when the user explicitly asks to run, see, or set up their morning brief, or if they invoke /morning by name. A question about their day, schedule, or calendar is not by itself a request for the brief; answer it directly instead.\n- anthropic-skills:pdf: Use this skill whenever the user wants to do anything with PDF files. This includes reading or extracting text/tables from PDFs, combining or merging multiple PDFs into one, splitting PDFs apart, rotating pages, adding watermarks, creating new PDFs, filling PDF forms, encrypting/decrypting PDFs, extracting images, and OCR on scanned PDFs to make them searchable. If the user mentions a .pdf file or asks to produce one, use this skill.\n- anthropic-skills:pptx: Use this skill any time a .pptx or .potx file is involved in any way — as input, output, or both. This includes: creating slide decks, pitch decks, or presentations; reading, parsing, or extracting text from any .pptx or .potx file (even if the extracted content will be used elsewhere, like in an email or summary); editing, modifying, or updating existing presentations; combining or splitting slide files; working with templates (.potx), layouts, speaker notes, or comments. Trigger whenever the user mentions \"deck,\" \"slides,\" \"presentation,\" or references a .pptx or .potx filename, regardless of what they plan to do with the content afterward. If a .pptx or .potx file needs to be opened, created, or touched, use this skill.\n- anthropic-skills:schedule: Create or update a scheduled task that runs automatically. Use when the user says things like \"every day\", \"each morning\", \"remind me in an hour\", \"run this at noon\", or wants to reschedule an existing task.\n- anthropic-skills:setup-cowork: Guided Cowork setup — install role-matched plugins, connect your tools, try a skill.\n- anthropic-skills:skill-creator: Create new skills, modify and improve existing skills, and measure skill performance. Use when users want to create a skill from scratch, edit, or optimize an existing skill, run evals to test a skill, benchmark skill performance with variance analysis, or optimize a skill's description for better triggering accuracy.\n- anthropic-skills:xlsx: Use this skill any time a spreadsheet file is the primary input or output. This means any task where the user wants to: open, read, edit, or fix an existing .xlsx, .xlsm, .xltx, .csv, or .tsv file (e.g., adding columns, computing formulas, formatting, charting, cleaning messy data); create a new spreadsheet from scratch or from other data sources; or convert between tabular file formats. Trigger especially when the user references a spreadsheet file by name or path — even casually (like \"the xlsx in my downloads\") — and wants something done to it or produced from it. Also trigger for cleaning or restructuring messy tabular data files (malformed rows, misplaced headers, junk data) into proper spreadsheets. The deliverable must be a spreadsheet file. Do NOT trigger when the primary deliverable is a Word document, HTML report, standalone Python script, database pipeline, or Google Sheets API integration, even if tabular data is involved.\n- dataviz: Use this skill whenever you are about to create ANY chart, graph, plot, dashboard, or data visualization, in ANY output medium — an HTML or React artifact, inline SVG, plotting code in any library (matplotlib, plotly, d3, Recharts, …), an image/PNG you will render and upload, or a chart shared into Slack. Read it BEFORE writing the first line of chart code, choosing chart colors, building a stat tile / meter / KPI row, or laying out a dashboard. Produces visualizations that read as one system — elegant, accessible, consistent in light and dark — using a brand-neutral placeholder palette you swap for your own. Teaches a design-system-agnostic method: a form heuristic, a color formula with a runnable validator, mark specs, and interaction rules. A validated default palette is documented in `references/palette.md` — swap that file's values for your brand's. Triggers on: \"chart\", \"graph\", \"plot\", \"data viz\", \"visualization\", \"dashboard\", \"analytics\", \"visualize data\", \"categorical colors\", \"sequential / diverging palette\", \"stat tile\", \"sparkline\", \"heatmap\", \"legend\", \"axis\", \"tooltip\", \"chart colors\", \"color by series\".\n- artifact-design: Design guidance and fundamentals for Artifacts.\n- artifact-diagramming: Diagramming know-how for Artifacts — when a picture earns its place, how to draw one that shows the real mechanism, and the inline-SVG mechanics that keep it legible in both themes.\n- artifact-capabilities: Runtime capabilities a published Artifact page can be granted — behavior static HTML cannot provide on its own, such as the page reading live or connected data, keeping state shared across viewers, or updating and republishing itself. Serves this user's live capability roster and the typed call definitions. Load it whenever the user asks for an artifact needing any such runtime behavior.\n- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review the changed code for reuse, simplification, efficiency, and altitude cleanups, then apply the fixes. Quality only — it does not hunt for bugs; use /code-review for that.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled cloud agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring cloud agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Reference for the Claude API / Anthropic SDK — model ids, pricing, params, streaming, tool use, MCP, agents, caching, token counting, model migration.\nTRIGGER — read BEFORE opening the target file; don't skip because it \"looks like a one-liner\" — whenever: the prompt names Claude/Anthropic in any form (Claude, Anthropic, Fable, Opus, Sonnet, Haiku, `anthropic`, `@anthropic-ai`, `claude-*`, `us.anthropic.*`, `[1m]`); the user asks about an LLM (pricing/model choice/limits/caching) — never answer from memory; OR the task is LLM-shaped with provider unstated (agent/MCP/tool-definition/multi-agent/RAG/LLM-judge/computer-use; generate/summarize/extract/classify/rewrite/converse over NL; debugging refusals/cutoffs/streaming/tool-calls/tokens).\nSKIP only when another provider is being worked on (overrides all triggers): OpenAI/GPT/Gemini/Llama/Mistral/Cohere/Ollama named in the query; OR `grep -rE 'openai|langchain_openai|google.generativeai|genai|mistralai|cohere|ollama'` over the project hits (run this grep FIRST if no provider named — don't Read the file).\n- run: Launch and drive this project's app to see a change working. Use when asked to run, start, or screenshot the app, or to confirm a change works in the real app (not just tests). First looks for a project skill that already covers launching the app; otherwise falls back to built-in patterns per project type (CLI, server, TUI, Electron, browser-driven, library).\n- init: Node and Answer wire records now require\n12\t`relevant_as_of` and the closed current staleness projection\n13\t`FRESH | UNDER_REVIEW | STALE | ARCHIVED_REVIVED`. `ServeRepository` folds the\n14\tcurrent trigger, clock, and state streams on every Answer and Node read; it\n15\tadds `STALE` for a fired revision trigger and `UNDER-REVIEW` for an expired\n16\tclock or revived archive without mutating a frozen answer.\n17\t\n18\tThe production `job:liveness-sweep` entry point reads each run-pinned register\n19\tversion, fails loudly when `livenessPolicy` or its `standard` class member is\n20\tabsent, creates provenance-bearing class review clocks, detects real recorded\n21\tprovider `model_version` transitions from `ledger.raw_artifact`, emits\n22\t`honesty.staleness_trigger_fired`, and archives only after the register window\n23\twhen no fired trigger remains open. A repeat sweep cannot append a second\n24\tarchive event without an intervening query.\n25\t\n26\tRun creation atomically records the new query at database time, while\n27\t`POST /v1/asks` records exact-question revisit activity within the same asker\n28\tboundary before accepting a new run. If the prior graph is archived, it appends\n29\t`ARCHIVED_REVIVED` per retained Answer/Node plus a typed `REVIVED` question\n30\tevent. No graph row is deleted or rewritten. `UNDER-EXPLORED` is deliberately\n31\tabsent from the retirement predicate.\n32\t\n33\tThe child-to-parent affected-set and execution seam is pure and tested:\n34\tmachine arithmetic receives only the changed node plus ancestors and the\n35\trecorded arrow order, then the model callback is invoked for exactly those\n36\tsame nodes. The honesty report leaves these two callback-composition helpers\n37\t`UNATTACHED`; the shipped scheduler attaches recorded-trigger detection,\n38\tstate/event emission, read projection, clocks, retirement, and revival, but it\n39\tdoes not manufacture model calls merely to turn the callback seam green.\n40\t\n41\t## Inventory\n42\t\n43\t- Domain and persistence behavior: `packages/liveness/src/index.ts`.\n44\t- Register authority: `packages/register/src/index.ts` and\n45\t  `packages/register/src/runtime-environment.ts`.\n46\t- Scheduler entry point: `apps/scheduler/src/index.ts`,\n47\t  `apps/scheduler/src/cli.ts`, and root `job:liveness-sweep`.\n48\t- Pull and push surfaces: `packages/serve/src/index.ts`,\n49\t  `apps/api/src/index.ts`, and `packages/contract/src/index.ts`.\n50\t- Persistence: `migrations/0014_s11.sql` and complete Drizzle mirrors in\n51\t  `packages/db/src/schema.ts`.\n52\t- Tests: `tests/unit/liveness-s11.test.ts`,\n53\t  `tests/unit/register-s11.test.ts`,\n54\t  `tests/architecture/s11-contract.test.ts`, and the S11 block in\n55\t  `tests/integration/database.test.ts`.\n56\t- Honesty: `tools/orphan-audit/src/index.ts` and generated\n57\t  `reports/orphan-audit.json`.\n58\t\n59\t## Carrier and behavior evidence\n60\t\n61\t- `core.revision_trigger` is an ordered WATCHING/FIRED/RESOLVED event stream;\n62\t  provider version changes are detected only from persisted raw artifacts.\n63\t- `core.review_clock` prints question class, due time, register key/version,\n64\t  and source reference. No production TTL or retirement number exists outside\n65\t  a supplied row.\n66\t- `core.staleness_state` is an append-only state event stream, including the\n67\t  internal archival state; the wire vocabulary remains the ruled four-member\n68\t  projection.\n69\t- `core.question_liveness_event` records QUERY, ARCHIVED, and REVIVED. All four\n70\t  new tables reject UPDATE/DELETE and carry explicit runtime/replay grants.\n71\t- `core.node.relevant_as_of` and `serve.answer.relevant_as_of` are stamped by\n72\t  PostgreSQL at spawn and returned on reads.\n73\t- `honesty.staleness_trigger_fired` carries projection-grade trigger identity\n74\t  and affected subjects; The run legitimately had more than\n155\t180 days between its recorded test query and the pinned 2030 sweep instant.\n156\tThe provider gateway had historically stored each OpenAI-compatible response\n157\t`id` (a per-request completion identifier) in `model_version`. StoredStalenessState;\n35\t    readonly atSequence: number;\n36\t  }[];\n37\t}): StalenessProjection {\n38\t  if (!Number.isFinite(input.relevantAsOf.getTime()) || !Number.isFinite(input.now.getTime())) {\n39\t    throw new TypedDomainError(\"LIVENESS_TIME_INVALID\", \"Staleness projection requires valid instants\");\n40\t  }\n41\t  const latestByTrigger = new Map<string, (typeof input.triggerEvents)[number]>();\n42\t  for (const event of [...input.triggerEvents].sort((a, b) => a.atSequence - b.atSequence)) {\n43\t    latestByTrigger.set(event.triggerKey, event);\n44\t  }\n45\t  const fired = [...latestByTrigger.values()].some((event) => event.state === \"FIRED\");\n46\t  const latestState = [...input.stateEvents].sort((a, b) => b.atSequence - a.atSequence)[0];\n47\t  if (latestState?.state === \"ARCHIVED_REVIVED\") {\n48\t    return { state: \"ARCHIVED_REVIVED\", badge: \"UNDER-REVIEW\", relevantAsOf: input.relevantAsOf.toISOString(), basis: \"RECORDED_STATE\" };\n49\t  }\n50\t  if (latestState?.state === \"STALE\") {\n51\t    return { state: \"STALE\", badge: \"STALE\", relevantAsOf: input.relevantAsOf.toISOString(), basis: (latestState?.state === \"UNDER_REVIEW\" || latestState?.state === \"ARCHIVED\") {\n57\t    return { state: \"UNDER_REVIEW\", badge: \"UNDER-REVIEW\", relevantAsOf: input.relevantAsOf.toISOString(), basis: \"RECORDED_STATE\" };\n58\t  }\n59\t  if (input.reviewDueAt !== null && input.now.getTime() >= input.reviewDueAt.getTime()) {\n60\t    return { state: \"UNDER_REVIEW\", badge: \"UNDER-REVIEW\", relevantAsOf: input.relevantAsOf.toISOString(), basis: \"TTL_EXPIRED\" };\n61\t  }\n62\t  return { state: \"FRESH\", badge: null, relevantAsOf: input.relevantAsOf.toISOString(), basis: \"SNAPSHOT\" };\n63\t}\n64\t\n65\texport interface AffectedReassessmentPlan {\n66\t  readonly affectedNodeIds: readonly string[];\n67\t  readonly rejudgeNodeIds: readonly string[];\n68\t}\n69\t\n70\texport function planAffectedReassessment(\n71\t  changedNodeId: string,\n72\t  nodes: readonly { readonly nodeId: string; readonly parentNodeId: string | null }[]\n73\t): AffectedReassessmentPlan {\n74\t  const parentByNode = new Map(nodes.map((node) => [node.nodeId, node.parentNodeId]));\n75\t  if (!parentByNode.has(changedNodeId)) throw new TypedDomainError(\"LIVENESS_NODE_NOT_FOUND\", changedNodeId);\n76\t  const affected: string[] = [];\n77\t  const seen = new Set<string>();\n78\t  let cursor: string | null = changedNodeId;\n79\t  while (cursor !== null) {\n80\t    if }\n85\t  return Object.freeze({ affectedNodeIds: Object.freeze(affected), rejudgeNodeIds: Object.freeze([...affected]) });\n86\t}\n87\t\n88\texport async function propagateAffectedNodes(\n89\t  plan: TypedDomainError(\"LIVENESS_THRESHOLD_INVALID\", \"Retirement window must be register-supplied and positive\");\n109\t  }\n110\t  if (input.hasOpenRevisionTrigger) return { kind: \"KEEP\", reason: \"OPEN_REVISION_TRIGGER\" };\n111\t  if (input.now.getTime() - input.lastQueriedAt.getTime() < input.policy.retireAfterMs) {\n112\t    return { kind: \"KEEP\", reason: \"RECENT_QUERY\" };\n113\t  }\n114\t  // UNDER-EXPLORED is intentionally not consulted: isolation is never a retirement cause.\n115\t  return { kind: \"ARCHIVE\" };\n116\t}\n117\t\n118\texport class LivenessRepository {\n119\t );\n159\t      }\n160\t      return matches.rows.length;\n161\t    });\n162\t  }\n163\t\n164\t  async recordTriggerFired(input: {\n165\t    readonly runId: string;\n166\t    readonly triggerKey: 1 FROM core.review_clock AS clock\n293\t           WHERE clock.run_id=subject.run_id AND clock.subject_kind=subject.subject_kind\n294\t             AND clock.subject_ref=subject.subject_ref AND async sweep(now: Date, policy: LivenessPolicyReceipt): Promise<readonly string[]> {\n345\t    await this.detectProviderModelVersionTriggers();\n346\t    await this.ensureReviewClocks(policy);\n347\t    const runs = await this.pool.query<{\n348\t      run_id: string;\n349\t      as_of: Date;\n350\t      has_open_trigger: boolean;\n351\t      latest_liveness_kind: string | null;\n352\t    }>(\n353\t      `SELECT run.run_id, max(query.occurred_at) AS as_of,\n354\t              EXISTS (\n355\t                SELECT 1 FROM (\n356\t                  SELECT DISTINCT ON (trigger_key) trigger_key, state\n357\t                  FROM core.revision_trigger WHERE run_id=run.run_id ORDER BY trigger_key, at_seq DESC\n358\t                ) latest WHERE latest.state = 'FIRED'\n359\t              ) AS has_open_trigger,\n360\t              (SELECT kind FROM core.question_liveness_event\n361\t               WHERE run_id=run.run_id ORDER BY at_seq DESC LIMIT 1) AS latest_liveness_kind\n362\t       FROM core.run AS run\n363\t       LEFT JOIN core.question_liveness_event AS query ON query.run_id=run.run_id AND query.kind='QUERY'\n364\t       WHERE run.register_version=$1\n365\t       GROUP BY run.run_id\n366\t       HAVING max(query.occurred_at) IS NOT NULL\n367\t       ORDER BY run.created_at_seq`,\n368\t      [policy.registerVersion]\n369\t    );\n370\t    const archived: string[] = [];\n371\t    for (const run of runs.rows) {\n372\t      if (run.latest_liveness_kind === \"ARCHIVED\") continue;\n373\t      if (decideRetirement({ now, lastQueriedAt: run.as_of, policy, hasOpenRevisionTrigger: run.has_open_trigger, underExplored: false }).kind !== \"ARCHIVE\") continue;\n374\t      await withWriteTransaction(this.pool, async (client) => {\n375\t        const subjects = await client.query<{ subject_kind: \"ANSWER\" | \"NODE\"; subject_ref: string }>(\n376\t          `SELECT 'ANSWER'::text AS subject_kind, answer_id::text AS subject_ref FROM serve.answer WHERE run_id=$1\n377\t           UNION ALL SELECT 'NODE'::text, node_id::text FROM core.node WHERE run_id=$1`,\n378\t          [run.run_id]\n379\t        );\n380\t        for (const subject of subjects.rows) await client.query(\n381\t          `INSERT INTO core.staleness_state (run_id, subject_kind, subject_ref, state, reason, at_seq)\n382\t           VALUES ($1,$2,$3,'ARCHIVED','COMPOSITE_RETIREMENT',$4)`,\n383\t          [run.run_id, subject.subject_kind, subject.subject_ref, await allocateSequence(client)]\n384\t        );\n385\t        await client.query(\n386\t          `INSERT INTO core.question_liveness_event (run_id, kind, occurred_at, at_seq)\n387\t           VALUES ($1,'ARCHIVED',$2,$3)`,\n388\t          [run.run_id, now, await allocateSequence(client)]\n389\t        );\n390\t      });\n391\t      archived.push(run.run_id);\n392\t    }\n393\t    return Object.freeze(archived);\n394\t  }\n395\t}\n396\t"}]},"uuid":"8347dd29-f9ba-4b27-b100-71fe71251b38","timestamp":"2026-08-08T19:52:47.983Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"0ca8c298-c89b-49e7-92e0-932e995a221f","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/vladmihaimiron/Documents/DebateAI-V3","sessionId":"a8fcf2e3-bdd1-4a10-bcd6-3ec887012907","version":"2.1.221","gitBranch":"main","slug":"hidden-riding-bengio"}

S11 staleness and liveness: DR-015/016, AC-05/64/72.\n2\tALTER TABLE core.node\n3\t  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS relevant_as_of timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT clock_timestamp();\n4\tALTER TABLE serve.answer\n5\t  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS relevant_as_of timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT clock_timestamp();\n6\t\n7\tCREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS core.revision_trigger (\n8\t  revision_trigger_event_id uuid PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),\n9\t  run_id uuid NOT NULL REFERENCES core.run(run_id),\n10\t  trigger_key text NOT NULL CHECK (length(btrim(trigger_key)) > 0),\n11\t  trigger_kind text NOT NULL CHECK (trigger_kind IN ('WATCHED_CONDITION', 'PROVIDER_MODEL_VERSION', 'CONTRADICTS_PRIOR')),\n12\t  subject_kind text NOT NULL CHECK (subject_kind IN ('ANSWER', 'NODE')),\n13\t  subject_ref text NOT NULL CHECK (length(btrim(subject_ref)) > 0),\n14\t  state text NOT NULL CHECK (state IN ('WATCHING', 'FIRED', 'RESOLVED')),\n15\t  reason text NOT NULL CHECK (length(btrim(reason)) > 0),\n16\t  at_seq bigint NOT NULL UNIQUE CHECK (at_seq > 0)\n17\t);\n18\t\n19\tCREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS core.review_clock (\n20\t  review_clock_event_id uuid PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),\n21\t  run_id uuid NOT NULL REFERENCES core.run(run_id),\n22\t  subject_kind text NOT NULL CHECK (subject_kind IN ('ANSWER', 'NODE')),\n23\t  subject_ref text NOT NULL CHECK (length(btrim(subject_ref)) > 0),\n24\t  question_class text NOT NULL CHECK (length(btrim(question_class)) > 0),\n25\t  due_at timestamptz NOT NULL,\n26\t  register_row_key text NOT NULL CHECK (register_row_key = 'livenessPolicy'),\n27\t  register_version bigint NOT NULL CHECK (register_version > 0),\n28\t  register_source_ref text NOT NULL CHECK (length(btrim(register_source_ref)) > 0),\n29\t  at_seq bigint NOT NULL UNIQUE CHECK (at_seq > 0)\n30\t);\n31\t\n32\tCREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS core.staleness_state (\n33\t  staleness_state_event_id uuid PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),\n34\t  run_id uuid NOT NULL REFERENCES core.run(run_id),\n35\t  subject_kind text NOT NULL CHECK (subject_kind IN ('ANSWER', 'NODE')),\n36\t  subject_ref text NOT NULL CHECK (length(btrim(subject_ref)) > 0),\n37\t  state text NOT NULL CHECK (state IN ('FRESH', 'UNDER_REVIEW', 'STALE', 'ARCHIVED', 'ARCHIVED_REVIVED')),\n38\t  reason text NOT NULL CHECK (length(btrim(reason)) > 0),\n39\t  at_seq bigint NOT NULL UNIQUE CHECK (at_seq > 0)\n40\t);\n41\t\n42\tCREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS core.question_liveness_event (\n43\t  question_liveness_event_id uuid PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),\n44\t  run_id uuid NOT NULL REFERENCES core.run(run_id),\n45\t  kind text NOT NULL CHECK (kind IN ('QUERY', 'ARCHIVED', 'REVIVED')),\n46\t  occurred_at timestamptz NOT NULL,\n47\t  at_seq bigint NOT NULL UNIQUE CHECK (at_seq > 0)\n48\t);\n49\t\n50\tALTER TABLE core.run_progress_event\n51\t  DROP CONSTRAINT IF EXISTS run_progress_event_kind_check,\n52\t  ADD CONSTRAINT run_progress_event_kind_check CHECK (\n53\t    kind IN ('ENVELOPE_CONSUMED', 'ENVELOPE_STATE', 'PHASE', 'TERMINAL', 'honesty.staleness_trigger_fired')\n54\t  );\n55\t\n56\tCREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS revision_trigger_subject_order\n57\t  ON core.revision_trigger (run_id, subject_kind, subject_ref, at_seq);\n58\tCREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS review_clock_subject_order\n59\t  ON core.review_clock (run_id, subject_kind, subject_ref, at_seq);\n60\tCREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS staleness_state_subject_order\n61\t  ON core.staleness_state (run_id, subject_kind, subject_ref, at_seq);\n62\tCREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS question_liveness_run_order\n63\t  ON core.question_liveness_event (run_id, at_seq);\n64\t\n65\tREVOKE ALL ON core.revision_trigger, core.review_clock, core.staleness_state,\n66\t  core.question_liveness_event FROM PUBLIC;\n67\tGRANT SELECT, INSERT ON core.revision_trigger, core.review_clock, core.staleness_state,\n68\t  core.question_liveness_event TO debateai_runtime;\n69\tGRANT SELECT ON core.revision_trigger, core.review_clock, core.staleness_state,\n70\t  core.question_liveness_event TO debateai_replay;\n71\t\n72\tDO $$\n73\tDECLARE table_name text;\n74\tBEGIN\n75\t  FOREACH table_name IN ARRAY I'd classify this as non-blocking, arguably correct behavior.\n\nCould a caller supply an ancient as_of to force a run stale? No — max() always takes the largest occurred_at, and startRun already recorded one at real creation time, so an ancient as_of would be ignored. An ARCHIVED internal state maps to UNDER_REVIEW in the projection, which seems intentional — archived content displays as \"under review\" until it's revived, even though the wire vocabulary only includes FRESH, UNDER_REVIEW, STALE, and ARCHIVED_REVIVED. {"parentUuid":"5ba100a1-06ba-412c-b479-4eb873695176","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a5a3f8308606d29b3","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-8","id":"msg_011CdqsZC1rn9VM5iLmMa8VV","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"thinking","thinking":"I'm stepping back to review the design holistically for any remaining issues across the architectural principles and decision records, particularly around DR-115 and the recordQuery revival with caller-supplied timestamps. The key concern here is whether using the caller's as_of value could be exploited to force archival, but the max() logic prevents that and the feature genuinely tracks query activity, so it's not a blocking issue.\n\nI do want to flag one remaining detail though: in the API layer, recordQuery uses the caller's declared as_of timestamp for the QUERY event, which technically means caller-controlled content is still in the retirement path. This is a minor observation since the whole S11 rework was about removing caller influence from retirement logic, but this timestamp only ever extends liveness rather than forcing staleness, so it's worth noting as non-blocking.\n\nNow I'm examining the foldStaleness precedence rules—specifically how a recorded STALE state event interacts with subsequent FIRED and RESOLVED events when a trigger fires and then gets resolved. This might be a bug or an intentional design choice. The staleness_state stream records STALE as a permanent marker, and resolving the trigger doesn't append a new state event to clear it, so the content remains STALE indefinitely until archived or revived. The question is whether this is acceptable under the domain model—perhaps it's intentional that staleness persists until a new answer version is served, but there's no clear path back to FRESH once a trigger fires.\n\nActually, looking at how `foldStaleness` works with the trigger lifecycle, this is coherent: the STALE state event was recorded and never rescinded, so it holds regardless of whether the trigger is now RESOLVED. The trigger's lifecycle (WATCHING→FIRED→RESOLVED) is separate from the staleness_state ledger, so the design is consistent with the append-only event model—it's not a bug, just a design choice worth noting.\n\nFor the archival flow specifically, this doesn't cause problems either. When the lifecycle test resolves the trigger and sweeps, the archival check looks at whether there's an open revision trigger by querying for FIRED states. After resolution, the latest trigger state is RESOLVED, so `has_open_trigger` becomes false and archival proceeds normally. The recorded STALE state doesn't block archival since the archival decision keys on the trigger state and query status, not the staleness ledger.\n\nThe sticky STALE is really just a read-projection quirk—arguably correct since the content genuinely is stale until it gets re-served. Not a blocker.\n\nI'm also noticing that `decideRetirement` always receives `underExplored` as false from the sweep call, and the unit test confirms this parameter never influences the outcome. It's a vestigial parameter since the comment explicitly rules that UNDER-EXPLORED can't trigger retirement. That said, keeping it as an explicitly ignored input with the comment actually documents the design intent, so it's defensible to leave as-is.\n\nThe liveness domain itself is well-structured: pure functions handling the core logic, a clean repository layer, typed domain errors, and discriminated unions for the state and retirement kinds. The projection basis is properly typed. Append-only is confirmed through migration triggers and all insert paths. The ADTs are solid across stored and projected staleness states plus retirement results. Migration 0014 is the DDL source of truth with drizzle mirroring it, and there's an architecture test validating parity. Attestations are next on the checklist.\n\nNow I'm checking whether the persisted model_version at the database level matches what's returned—the provider unit test asserts the returned modelVersion I ran this myself against real PostgreSQL in my sandbox environment, so this is direct confirmation rather than relying on the orchestrator's claim.\n\nNow I have the complete picture for my final review: the SOLID principles are well-applied with clean separation and typed errors, the DDD approach correctly models staleness as a derived projection from event streams rather than a stored flag, and the TDD methodology is documented in the progress log showing the RED→GREEN cycle.\n\nThe key patterns are all in place—derived status via fold operations, append-only event ledgers with mutation rejection, algebraic data types, proper DDL authority with drizzle parity, and honest event provenance. The DR-115 requirements are met: no scaffolded data, retirement requires actual recorded QUERY events, and the model version now correctly reflects the response model identity. Everything checks out.\n\nThere are a couple of minor findings worth noting. The `underExplored` parameter in the retirement decision function is never actually used—the sweep always passes false and the function ignores it beyond a comment, so it's just vestigial code that could be cleaned up or kept as documentation of the isolation rule. More interesting is how staleness state works: once a STALE event is recorded, it sticks around even after the trigger resolves, because the trigger resolution only updates the trigger table, not the staleness state itself. The staleness clears on re-serve instead, which appears intentional and doesn't impact archival logic since that keys off trigger state and query age anyway.\n\nThere's also a timestamp detail in the query recording—it uses the caller-supplied timestamp rather than database time, which means it can only extend liveness windows, never force early archival, so there's no risk of fabricating stale states. That's the last place in the liveness path where caller content influences timestamps.\n\nNone of these are blocking issues, so the verdict is approved. Now I'm double-checking whether there's a genuine blocking problem I might have overlooked, particularly around model versioning. The RED test at line 84 would've caught the old behavior where id was stored instead of model — it expects the model value but would've gotten the id. The initial QUERY event is written atomically at `clock_timestamp()` inside `RunRepository.startRun` (`packages/db/src/index.ts:151-155`). The lifecycle test archives by pinning the sweep instant to 2030 against a genuinely recorded ~2026 QUERY event (`tests/integration/database.test.ts:865-873`), the trigger resolved first — a real staleness reference, not a wall-clock hack. Verified: the happy-path run is created via `startRun` (`database.test.ts:94`), so the event the sweep requires genuinely exists.\n\n**model_version provenance fix (honest S04 close).** The gateway now persists `candidate.data.model` (`packages/providers/src/index.ts:210`) and returns `responseJson.model` (`:243`) — the response model identity — leaving the request `id` only in the raw response body. The RED is real: `tests/unit/provider.test.ts:40-84` sends `id: \"fixture-response\"`, `model: \"fixture-maker/fixture-model\"` and asserts `modelVersion === \"fixture-maker/fixture-model\"` (would have returned the id before the fix). `detectProviderModelVersionTriggers` (`:302`) reacts only to real recorded `model_version` transitions in `ledger.raw_artifact`, so ordinary request traffic no longer spoofs version churn.\n\n**Append-only.** `migrations/0014_s11.sql` installs `reject_mutation` BEFORE UPDATE/DELETE triggers on all four carriers; every state change INSERTs a typed event. I watched the DELETE rejection fire live (`staleness_state rejects DELETE`) and the graph node count is byte-identical across archive+revival (`database.test.ts:879-887`).\n\n**AC-76 — no invented numbers.** `readLivenessPolicy` (`packages/register/src/index.ts:116`) sources `review_after_ms`/`retire_after_ms` from `register.register_row`, failing loud (`LIVENESS_POLICY_UNRESOLVED/_INVALID/_PROVENANCE_MISSING`); `decideRetirement` throws `LIVENESS_THRESHOLD_INVALID` on non-positive windows. The 180-day figure in tests is a labeled test-layer receipt only.\n\n**Honesty rows machine-derived.** `tools/orphan-audit/src/index.ts:369` bans hand-authored `attachment:\"ATTACHED\"|\"UNATTACHED\"` literals; `:453` derives attachment from the reachability walk.
